CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_BEGIN -->\n## Summary\n\n`tools/qlab/test_aqft.py` and `tools/qlab/test_aqft_fix.py` were sweeps with no assertions: a crash-free run was a pass. They are now pytest tests that `aqft.py` must satisfy:\n\n- normalization and unitarity (`|U†U−I|`, statevector norm, `inverse(aqft)` round-trip, and `aqft(n,n)` equal to `qft(n)`)\n- ideal fidelity in `[0, 1]`, non-decreasing in `m`, and 1 at `m=n` (every basis state for n=2..5, plus the seed-5 sample)\n- gate and lowered 2-qubit counts non-decreasing in `m` and bounded by the exact QFT, for n=1..10\n- bit-identical repeats at a fixed seed\n- `examples/aqft5.json` (`[\"AQFT\",[5,4]]`) expands to the drop-rule circuit (16 gates, 9 CP, 24 lowered 2q)\n\nCurrent outputs are frozen in `tools/qlab/baselines/aqft.json`. Regenerate with:\n\n```\nOMP_NUM_THREADS=1 python3 tools/qlab/baselines/measure_aqft.py\n```\n\nThat script reruns the old seeds on a slice that finishes in about 1 s: ideal fidelity seed 5, n=3..6, 8 inputs; noisy fidelity seed 11, n=3..5, 4 inputs, `p1=1e-3`, `p2=1e-2`, `gamma=1e-3`; phase estimation seed 55, t=5, 10 phases, m in {5, 3, 2}. The original n≤10 sweep (484 s) and the n=9,10 fix rerun (part of 327 s) are not recomputed; those rows stay in `RESULTS.md`.\n\n`python3 -m qlab test` now runs both files. `aqft.py` is unchanged.\n\n## Before / after\n\nMeasured on the existing RESULTS.md protocol and on `examples/aqft5.json` (neither sample was constructed for this change). numpy 1.26.4, `OMP_NUM_THREADS=1`. Because `aqft.py` is unchanged, before and after are the same numbers. There is no measurable improvement in approximation quality.\n\n| quantity | RESULTS.md | remeasured (before = after) |\n|---|---|---|\n| ideal F, n=4 m=3, seed 5, 8 inputs | 0.990485 | 0.990485 |\n| ideal F, n=5 m=3 | 0.959550 | 0.959550 |\n| ideal F, n=6 m=3 | 0.906327 | 0.906327 |\n| ideal min, n=5 m=1 | 0.000027 | 0.000027 |\n| noisy F, n=3 m=3, seed 11, 4 inputs | 0.8676 | 0.867631 |\n| noisy F, n=4 m=3 vs m=4 | 0.7609 vs 0.7578 | 0.760872 vs 0.757793 |\n| noisy F, n=5 m=3 vs m=5 | 0.7012 vs 0.6686 | 0.701228 vs 0.668578 |\n| PE t=5 exact ideal / noisy, seed 55, 10 phases | 0.7860±0.1656 / 0.4403±0.0888 | 0.785997±0.165640 / 0.440258±0.088810 |\n| PE t=5 m=3 ideal / noisy | 0.7488±0.1729 / 0.4581±0.1028 | 0.748829±0.172943 / 0.458088±0.102757 |\n| `aqft5.json` \\|0⟩ fidelity vs exact QFT | — | 0.9999999999999996 |\n| lowered 2q for AQFT[5,4] | 24 | 24 |\n| max \\|U†U−I\\|, n=2..4, all m | — | 8.88e-16 |\n\nSuite runtime: 10 passed in 5.3 s (`pytest tools/qlab/test_aqft.py tools/qlab/test_aqft_fix.py`). Inside `python3 -m qlab test`: `test_aqft.py` 0.7 s, `test_aqft_fix.py` 5.0 s. `ALL PASS` for the full qlab suite. Well under 60 s.\n\n## Broken `aqft` (not committed)\n\nReplacing `aqft()` with an inverted cutoff, a non-unitary phase, and an unseeded angle:\n\n```python\ndef aqft(n, m):\n    c = [g for g in qft(n) if not (g[0] == 'CP' and g[2] > 2 * np.pi / 2**m)]\n    c.append(('P', [0], 0.25j + 0.01 * np.random.random()))\n    return c\n```\n\n`pytest tools/qlab/test_aqft.py tools/qlab/test_aqft_fix.py` then reported **10 failed in 4.30 s**, one failure per test:\n\n- `test_normalization_and_unitarity` — max \\|U†U−I\\| was 0.197, not &lt; 1e-9\n- `test_ideal_fidelity_monotonic_and_bounded` — per-input fidelity dipped as m increased\n- `test_gate_counts_monotone_and_bounded_by_qft` — circuit was not the drop-rule subsequence of `qft`\n- `test_deterministic_with_fixed_seed` — two seed-5 runs disagreed (0.791033 vs 0.791022)\n- `test_matches_aqft5_reference` — AQFT[5,4] gate list no longer matched the reference expansion\n- `test_pe_normalization_and_bounds` — inverse-AQFT inside phase estimation was not the spec circuit\n- `test_pe_ideal_ordered_noise_bounded` — exact ideal P(correct) 0.329 fell below the m=3 arm 0.433 (baseline exact is 0.786)\n- `test_noisy_fidelity_bounded` — `aqft(n,n)` was not `qft(n)`\n- `test_deterministic_pe_and_noisy_avg` — repeated noisy fidelities differed\n- `test_matches_fix_baseline` — PE exact ideal mean 0.330 vs baseline 0.786\n\n`aqft.py` was restored afterward and is not part of this diff. Re-run on the restored file: 10 passed.\n\n<!-- CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_END -->\n\n<div><a CURSOR_AGENT_PR_BODY_BEGIN -->\n## What changed\n\nSHEPHERD-RELAY stays design-only. This adds the offline, keyless gate only:\n\n- `tools/shepherd/sources.json` — one allowlist record each for arXiv RSS/Atom, Wikimedia dumps, arXiv OAI-PMH, MedlinePlus XML, PMC OA (`set=pmc-open`), the National Weather Service API, OpenStreetMap planet replication diffs, and GitHub public releases.\n- Each record stores the license, terms URL, and rate limit as published on the cited page. Where the publisher does not publish a number, the record says so.\n- `tools/shepherd/check_terms.py` — rejects a source that lacks a license, a terms URL, or a published rate limit, or that requires auth, carries a credential, or sets a rate-limit-evasion flag.\n- No network fetch, no scraping, no raising a published cap. Bundle signing and encryption are out of scope.\n\nAuthenticated quotas are not stored. Wikimedia Enterprise HTML dumps, NOAA token APIs, and MedlinePlus RSS (personal use only) are excluded in the record notes.\n\n## Tests\n\n`python3 tools/shepherd/test_check_terms.py` on this machine: 13 tests, OK, in 0.006s. The suite blocks `socket.socket.connect` and checks that the module does not import a client. `python3 tools/shepherd/check_terms.py` reports `accepted 8 rejected 0`.\n\n## Before / after\n\nMeasured on Gitbook `main` `bd1b475b678944a5118b524dfd93646da4e4e65b` (same SHA as `origin/main` at start). That tree has no `tools/shepherd`, so the previous machine-checked source count is **0**. After this change the checker accepts **8** allowlisted sources and rejects **0** of them.\n\nThere is **no measurable improvement** on an existing accuracy, recall, or latency number. No prior gate existed, and the rejection cases in the unit tests were written for this change, so they are not an external score.\n\n## Limits\n\nOpen PRs at start did not touch `tools/shepherd` (#45, #40, #39). Summary\nAdds `tools/qlab`, a toolkit that needs only numpy. It contains:\n- a statevector simulator (up to 20 qubits)\n- circuit optimizers v1–v3\n- an approximate QFT\n- a calibrated noise twin (T1/T2, gate and readout errors, coupling-map routing)\n\nThe noise twin uses public IBM calibration snapshots (Apache-2.0, credited in `qlab/devices/NOTICE`). **Every number is a SIMULATION prediction, not a hardware measurement.** No result JSONs or caches are included.\n\n## Test\n`cd tools/qlab && OMP_NUM_THREADS=1 python3 -m qlab test` returns ALL PASS for test_sim, test_opt, test_noise, test_geom, test_opt2, test_opt3, selftest_twin and selftest_cli.\n\n## Gates\nModerator > Tessera > Security > CI/Build > Father's yes. Do not merge without Father's yes. CI is billing-blocked, so the local test run above is the gate.
